She just says my name, or maybe makes a little comment about what I'm doing. It's... well, it's clear enough that I've answered out loud before I realize there's no one actually there in the room with me. But... I don't know. It's just when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you felt your thoughts were being controlled, or that you were receiving special messages from the TV, radio, or other sources?\n\nPatient: No... not the television or the radio. And my thoughts are my own. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I mentioned a moment ago. The staff emails. And the morning announcements over the intercom. Even when the other teachers pull them up on their screens and gently show me that they went out to the whole staff... I just can't shake the feeling that they're actually coded messages meant for me. That's... that's the main reason I came in. I haven't noticed anything from the TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public—like in newspapers, on TV, or in conversations—are directed at you or have a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not newspapers or anything like that. Just... well, like I was just saying. The school. It's really just the morning announcements and those staff emails. I know my colleagues show me that everyone gets them, but... I still feel like they're coded messages about me. That's... that's really why I came in. Just that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual or strange thoughts that you couldn’t quite explain, even if they seemed to make sense to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. Just... what I've already mentioned to you. There's really nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Even though your colleagues show you that the emails and announcements are sent to everyone, do you still believe they are specifically meant for you in a way others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. I... I know they show me the screens, but... yes. I still feel like they're about me. That's... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things outside of school — like events in your neighborhood, things on the internet, or interactions with strangers — also have hidden meanings or are directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: No. Um... no. Just the school.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that things at school — like the emails and announcements — have meanings or connections that go beyond just your personal experience, such as involving larger events, organizations, or systems?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It's really just... about me. I haven't noticed anything to do with larger events, or... organizations. I don't think so. Just what happens at work. It's... well, it's clear enough that I've answered out loud before I realize there's no one actually there in the room with me. But... I don't know. It's just when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you felt your thoughts were being controlled, or that you were receiving special messages from the TV, radio, or other sources?\n\nPatient: No... not the television or the radio. And my thoughts are my own. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I mentioned a moment ago. The staff emails. And the morning announcements over the intercom. Even when the other teachers pull them up on their screens and gently show me that they went out to the whole staff... I just can't shake the feeling that they're actually coded messages meant for me. That's... that's the main reason I came in. I haven't noticed anything from the TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say or do in public—like in newspapers, on TV, or in conversations—are directed at you or have a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not newspapers or anything like that. Just... well, like I was just saying. The school. It's really just the morning announcements and those staff emails. I know my colleagues show me that everyone gets them, but... I still feel like they're coded messages about me. That's... that's really why I came in. Just that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual or strange thoughts that you couldn’t quite explain, even if they seemed to make sense to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. Just... what I've already mentioned to you. There's really nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Even though your colleagues show you that the emails and announcements are sent to everyone, do you still believe they are specifically meant for you in a way others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. I... I know they show me the screens, but... yes. I still feel like they're about me. That's... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things outside of school — like events in your neighborhood, things on the internet, or interactions with strangers — also have hidden meanings or are directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: No. Um... no. I don't think so. It's really just... about me. I haven't noticed anything to do with larger events, or... organizations. Just what I've already said.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that you know other people might find strange or impossible, even if they feel completely true to you?\n\nPatient: No. I... I don't think so. Just what happens at work. Nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts or beliefs that you know logically don’t make sense, but you still feel are true deep down?\n\nPatient: I... no. What I see happening makes sense to me. So... no, I don't have thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these experiences with the emails and announcements cause you a lot of distress or make it hard for you to go about your day, like teaching or interacting with colleagues?\n\nPatient: I... well, I'm still teaching. It's just... I guess I've needed some of my coworkers to cover parts of the project lessons lately. And... to just help me get through the planning meetings. \n\nAnd outside of school... I suppose I've stopped being very reliable for my book club. Or the theater... and the neighborhood events. I just... haven't really been up to it. But I am still teaching.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or experiences outside of school that others might not understand or seem strange, even if they feel completely true to you?\n\nPatient: No. I... I don't really know what you mean. It's just... school. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any other situations, even outside of school, where you felt messages or events were directed at you, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: No. I...
